(115)

A.7.3
Proof of the bound (
104
)

To continue we gather a few observations. First, since
\sinc(t):=\sin(t)/t=\int_{0}^{1}\cos(xt)dx
for every
t\neq 0
(and
\sinc(0)=1
) we have
\sinc^{\prime}(t)=\int_{0}^{1}-x\sin(xt)dx
hence
\max(|\sinc(t)|,|\sinc^{\prime}(t)|)\leq 1
. Now, by definition of the dual norm
\|\cdot\|_{a,\star}
, for every
\omega,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have
|\langle\omega,v\rangle|\leq\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\|v\|_{a}
.
Thus, by definition (
49
) of
C_{\kappa}
for every
0<t\leq R
we have

\forall v\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad|\alpha(t)\sin(t\langle\omega,v\rangle/2)/t|=|\alpha(t)\tfrac{\langle\omega,v\rangle}{2}\sinc(t\langle\omega,v\rangle/2)|\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\|v\|_{a}.

(116)

Recalling that
\bar{y}(u):=y+u(y^{\prime}-y)
,
\bar{n}(u):=n+u(n^{\prime}-n)
,
\bar{r}(u):=r+u(r^{\prime}-r)
, and
\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we now bound the following auxiliary functions and their derivatives, with arbitrary
\phi\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle g_{0,\phi}(u):=\cos(\langle\omega,\bar{y}(u)\rangle+\phi)\,,\ g_{1}(u):=\alpha(\bar{r}(u))\frac{\sin(\bar{r}(u)\langle\omega,n\rangle/2)}{\bar{r}(u)}\,,\ g_{2}(u):=\alpha(r^{\prime})\frac{\sin(r^{\prime}\langle\omega,\bar{n}(u)\rangle/2)}{r^{\prime}}\,.

Since
0<r^{\prime}\leq R
we have

\displaystyle|g^{\prime}_{0,\phi}(u)|

\displaystyle=|\sin(\langle\omega,\bar{y}(u)\rangle+\phi)\cdot\langle\omega,y^{\prime}-y\rangle|\leq|\langle\omega,y^{\prime}-y\rangle|\leq\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\cdot\|y^{\prime}-y\|_{a}\,,

\displaystyle|g^{\prime}_{2}(u)|

\displaystyle=|\alpha(r^{\prime})\cos(r^{\prime}\langle\omega,\bar{n}(u)\rangle/2)\cdot\tfrac{\langle\omega,n^{\prime}-n\rangle}{2}|\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}\cdot\|n^{\prime}-n\|_{a}\,.

As
g_{1}(u)=\alpha(\bar{r}(u))\frac{\langle\omega,n\rangle}{2}\sinc(\bar{r}(u)\langle\omega,n\rangle/2)
,
\|n\|_{a}=1
, and
\bar{r}(u)\leq\max(r,r^{\prime})\leq R
we get

\displaystyle|g^{\prime}_{1}(u)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\alpha^{\prime}(\bar{r}(u))\sinc(\bar{r}(u)\langle\omega,n\rangle/2)+\alpha(\bar{r}(u))\frac{\langle\omega,\cdot n\rangle}{2}\sinc^{\prime}(\bar{r}(u)\langle\omega,n\rangle/2)\Big|\cdot\Big|\frac{\langle\omega,n\rangle}{2}\cdot(r^{\prime}-r)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}\cdot(1+\|\omega\|_{a,\star}/2)\cdot|r^{\prime}-r|

Since
\|\bar{n}(u)\|_{a}\leq\max(\|n\|_{a},\|n^{\prime}\|_{a})=1
we also get using (
116
)

\max(|g_{1}(u)|,|g_{2}(u)|)\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\max(\|n\|_{a},\|\bar{n}(u)\|_{a})=\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\,.

We are now equipped to proceed.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{d}
.

Expressions (
105
)-(
106
) yield
f_{\mathrm{d},1}(u|\omega)=2g_{1}^{2}(u)
,
f_{\mathrm{d},2}(u|\omega)=2g_{2}^{2}(u)
. By (
52
) and the choice of
z_{1}=x,z_{2}=\tilde{x},z_{3}=x^{\prime}
we have
\Delta_{d}(z_{1},z_{2})=|r^{\prime}-r|
,
\Delta_{d}(z_{2},z_{3})=\|n^{\prime}-n\|_{a}
and we obtain the bound (
104
) since

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{d},1}(u)|

\displaystyle=|4g_{1}(u)g^{\prime}_{1}(u)|\leq C_{\kappa}(\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{2}+\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{3}/2)\cdot|r^{\prime}-r|\leq G(\omega)\cdot\Delta_{d}(z_{1},z_{2})\,,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{d},2}(u)|

\displaystyle=|4g_{2}(u)g^{\prime}_{2}(u)|\leq C_{\kappa}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{2}\cdot\|n^{\prime}-n\|_{a}\leq G(\omega)\cdot\Delta_{d}(z_{2},z_{3})\,.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{mm}
.

By the expression (
107
) we have
f_{\mathrm{mm},1}=g_{0}
. The bound (
104
) follows from

\displaystyle|f_{\mathrm{mm},1}^{\prime}(u)|

\displaystyle=|g^{\prime}_{0,0}(u)|\leq\|\omega_{j}\|_{a,\star}\|y^{\prime}-y\|_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:conditions_on_alpha}\&\eqref{eq:DefMetricMM}}}{{\leq}}G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{mm}}(z_{1},z_{2}).

The case
\ell=\mathrm{md}
.

By the identity
\cos(\theta-\pi/2)=\sin(\theta)
we have
f_{\mathrm{md},1}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefFmd1}}}{{=}}\sqrt{2}g_{1}(0)g_{0,\phi}(u)
with
\phi:=\langle\omega,x\rangle/2-\pi/2
, and by (
109
)
f_{\mathrm{md},2}(u)=\sqrt{2}g_{1}(u)\sin(\psi(u))
with
\psi(u):=\langle\omega,y^{\prime}+\bar{r}(u)n/2\rangle
, and
\psi^{\prime}(u)=\langle\omega,n\rangle(r^{\prime}-r)/2
. Combining with (
110
) we obtain the bound
(
104
) since

\displaystyle|f_{\mathrm{md},1}^{\prime}(u)|

\displaystyle=|\sqrt{2}g_{1}(0)g^{\prime}_{0,\phi}(u)|\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}}{\sqrt{2}}\|\omega_{j}\|_{a,\star}^{2}\|y^{\prime}-y\|_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle C_{\kappa}\geq 1\&\eqref{eq:DefMetricMD}}}{{\leq}}G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{md}}(z_{1},z_{2})\,,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{md},2}(u)|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}|g^{\prime}_{1}(u)\sin(\psi(u))+g_{1}(u)\cos(\psi(u))\langle\omega,n\rangle(r^{\prime}-r)/2|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}(1+\|\omega\|_{a,\star}/2)|r^{\prime}-r|+\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}|r^{\prime}-r|/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{\sqrt{2}}(1+\|\omega\|_{a,\star})\cdot|r^{\prime}-r|\leq G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{md}}(z_{2},z_{3})\,,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{md},3}(u)|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\alpha(r^{\prime})\sin(\langle\omega,y^{\prime}\rangle+r^{\prime}\langle\omega,\bar{n}(u)\rangle)\cdot\langle\omega,n^{\prime}-n\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\|n^{\prime}-n\|_{a}\leq G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{md}}(z_{3},z_{4})\,.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{dd}

Denote
g_{i,j}
,
i,j=1,2
the functions defined as
g_{i}
with
r_{j},r^{\prime}_{j}
, etc. instead of
r,r^{\prime}
etc.
By (
111
)
we have
f_{\mathrm{dd},1}(u)=2g_{1,1}(0)g_{1,2}(0)g_{0,\phi}(u)
with
\phi:=r_{2}n_{2}/2-r_{1}n_{1}/2
, and by (
112
),
f_{\mathrm{dd},2}(u)=2g_{1,1}(u)g_{1,2}(0)\cos(\psi(u))
with
\psi_{2}(u):=\langle\omega,y^{\prime}+r_{2}n_{2}/2\rangle-\bar{r}_{1}(u)\langle\omega,n_{1}\rangle/2
,
\psi_{2}^{\prime}(u)=-\langle\omega,n_{1}\rangle(r^{\prime}_{1}-r_{1})/2
hence

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{dd},1}(u)|

\displaystyle=|2g_{1,1}(0)g_{1,2}(0)g^{\prime}_{0,\phi}(u)|\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{3}\cdot\|y^{\prime}-y\|_{a}\leq G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{dd}}(z_{1},z_{2})\,,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{dd},2}(u)|

\displaystyle=|2g_{1,2}(0)|\cdot|g_{1,1}^{\prime}(u)\cos(\psi(u))+g_{1,1}(u)\sin(\psi(u))\langle\omega,n_{1}\rangle(r^{\prime}_{1}-r_{1})/2|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}(1+\|\omega\|_{a,\star}/2)|r^{\prime}_{1}-r_{1}|+\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}|r^{\prime}_{1}-r_{1}|/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}}{2}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{2}\left(1+3\|\omega\|_{a,\star}/4\right)|r^{\prime}_{1}-r_{1}|\leq G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{dd}}(z_{2},z_{3})\,.

Similarly by (
114
)
f_{\mathrm{dd},4}(u)=2g_{1,1}(1)g_{1,2}(u)\cos(\psi_{4}(u))
with
\psi_{4}(u):=\langle\omega,y^{\prime}-r^{\prime}_{1}n^{\prime}_{1}/2\rangle+\bar{r}_{2}(u)\langle\omega,n_{2}\rangle/2
and the same reasoning yields the same bound. This establishes the bound (
104
) for
t=1,2,4
.

By (
113
) and the identity
2\sin v\cos w=\sin(v+w)+\sin(v-w)
we write

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{dd},3}(u)

\displaystyle=g_{1,2}(0)\frac{\alpha(r^{\prime}_{1})}{r^{\prime}_{1}}2\sin\big(r^{\prime}_{1}\langle\omega,\bar{n}_{1}(u)\rangle/2\big)\cos\big(\langle\omega,y^{\prime}+r_{2}n_{2}/2-r^{\prime}_{1}\cdot\bar{n}_{1}(u)/2\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=g_{1,2}(0)\frac{\alpha(r^{\prime}_{1})}{r^{\prime}_{1}}\Big(\sin(\langle\omega,y^{\prime}+r_{2}n_{2}/2\rangle)+\sin(\underbrace{r^{\prime}_{1}\langle\omega,\bar{n}_{1}(u)\rangle-\langle\omega,y^{\prime}+r_{2}n_{2}/2\rangle}_{\psi_{3}(u)})\Big)

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{dd},3}(u)|

\displaystyle=|g_{1,2}(0)\frac{\alpha(r^{\prime}_{1})}{r^{\prime}_{1}}\cos(\psi_{3}(u))\psi_{3}^{\prime}(u)|\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}\frac{\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}}{r^{\prime}_{1}}|\psi_{3}^{\prime}(u)|=\frac{C_{\kappa}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}}{2}|\langle\omega,n^{\prime}_{1}-n_{1}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}\|\omega\|_{a,\star}^{2}}{2}\cdot\|n^{\prime}_{1}-n_{1}\|_{a}\leq G(\omega)\Delta_{\mathrm{dd}}(z_{3},z_{4}).

The same reasoning works from (
115
) for
t=5
. This establishes the bound (
104
) for
t=3,5
.

A.7.4
Proof of Proposition
21

We denote by
\mathbb{B}_{a}
(resp.
\mathbb{S}_{a}
) the unit ball (resp. unit sphere) with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{a}
,
R_{d}:=\sup_{x\in\Theta_{d}}\|x\|_{a}
, and
R_{\mathrm{mm}}:=\sup_{x\in\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}}\|x\|_{a}
. Observe that

\max(R_{d},R_{\mathrm{mm}})=\sup_{x\in\Theta_{d}\cup\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}}\|x\|_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:bound_gammma_d}-\eqref{eq:bound_gammma_mm}}}{{=}}\sup_{x\in\Theta-\Theta}\|x\|_{a}=\operatorname{diam}_{a}(\Theta)=:D.

An upper bound of
\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau)
.

Denote
I:=(0,R_{d}]\subseteq\mathbb{R}
. We will soon show that

\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau):=\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d},\Delta_{d},\tau)\leq\mathcal{N}(I,|\cdot|,\tau/2)\times\mathcal{N}\big(\mathbb{S}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tfrac{\tau}{2(R_{d}+1)}\big).

(117)

By Lemma A.1 in
(
Gribonval et al. 2021a
)
, since
\mathbb{S}_{a}\subset\mathbb{B}_{a}
, we have

\mathcal{N}\big(\mathbb{S}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tfrac{\tau}{2(R_{d}+1)}\big)\leq\mathcal{N}\big(\mathbb{B}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tfrac{\tau}{4(R_{d}+1)}\big)
.
Moreover, by Lemma 5.7 in
(
Wainwright 2019
)
, for every
\tau>0
the inequality
\mathcal{N}\big(\mathbb{B}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\frac{\tau}{(4(R_{d}+1)}\big)\leq\big(1+8(R_{d}+1)/\tau\big)^{d}
is valid.
Finally, since

\mathcal{N}(I,|\cdot|,\tau/2)\leq 1+2R_{d}/\tau\leq 1+8(R_{d}+1)/\tau
we obtain

\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau)\leq\big(1+8(R_{d}+1)/\tau\big)^{d+1}\leq\big(1+8(D+1)/\tau\big)^{d+1}.

We now establish (
117
). Denote
N_{1}:=\mathcal{N}(I,|\cdot|,\tau/2)
,
N_{2}:=\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{S}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tau/(2(R_{d}+1)))
, and consider
(r_{i})_{i\in[N_{1}]}
a covering of
I
with respect to
|\cdot|
at scale
\tau/2
and
(s_{i})_{i\in[N_{2}]}
a covering of
\mathbb{S}_{a}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{a}
at scale
\tau/(2(R_{d}+1))
. We show that the family
(r_{i}\cdot s_{j})_{(i,j)\in[N_{1}]\times[N_{2}]}
is a covering of
\Theta_{d}
with respect to the metric
\Delta_{d}
at scale
\tau
.
For this, consider an arbitrary
x\in\Theta_{d}
(recall the definition (
43
)) and define
r:=\|x\|_{a}
and
n:=x/r
. By definition of
R_{d}
and
I
we have
r\in I
, and
\|n\|_{a}\in\mathbb{S}_{a}
, hence there are
i\in[N_{1}]
,
j\in[N_{2}]
such that
|r-r_{i}|\leq\tau/2
and
\|n-s_{j}\|_{a}\leq\tau/(2(R_{d}+1))
.
To reach the conclusion we show that
\Delta_{d}(x,r_{i}s_{j})\leq\tau
. Indeed

\displaystyle\Delta_{d}(x,r_{i}s_{j})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{d}(rn,r_{i}s_{j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefMetricD}}}{{=}}\|rn-r_{i}s_{j}\|_{a}+\|n-s_{j}\|_{a}\leq\|rn-r_{i}n\|_{a}+\|r_{i}n-r_{i}s_{j}\|_{a}+\|n-s_{j}\|_{a}

\displaystyle\leq|r-r_{i}|\|n\|_{a}+(r_{i}+1)\|n-s_{j}\|_{a}\leq\tau/2+(R_{d}+1)\tau/(2(R_{d}+1))\leq\tau.

An upper bound of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau)
.

By definition of
R_{\mathrm{mm}}
we have
\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}\subset R_{\mathrm{mm}}\cdot\mathbb{B}_{a}
. By
(
Gribonval et al. 2021a
, Lemma A.1)

(
Wainwright 2019
, Lemma 5.7)
and (
53
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau):=\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}\big(R_{\mathrm{mm}}\cdot\mathbb{B}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tau/2\big)=\mathcal{N}\big(\mathbb{B}_{a},\|\cdot\|_{a},\tau/(2R_{\mathrm{mm}})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+4R_{\mathrm{mm}}/\tau\big)^{d}\leq\big(1+4D/\tau\big)^{d}.

An upper bound of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{md}}(\tau)
.

By (
45
), we have
\Theta_{\mathrm{md}}\subset\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}
. Thus by
(
Gribonval et al. 2021a
, Lemma A.1)
, we have

\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{md}}(\tau)=\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{\mathrm{md}},\Delta_{\mathrm{md}},\tau)\leq\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\Delta_{\mathrm{md}},\tau/2).

Now, given the definition (
54
) of
\Delta_{\mathrm{md}}

we have

\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{md}}(\tau)\leq\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\Delta_{\mathrm{md}},\tau/2)\leq\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d},\Delta_{d},\tau/4)\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\Delta_{\mathrm{mm}},\tau/4).

Indeed, with
(x_{i})_{i\in[\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau/4)]}
a covering of
\Theta_{d}
with respect to
\Delta_{d}
at scale
\tau/4
and
(y_{j})_{j\in[\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau/4)]}
a covering of
\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}
with respect to
\Delta_{\mathrm{mm}}
at scale
\tau/4
it is straightforward to show using (
54
) that
(x_{i},y_{j})_{(i,j)\in[\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau/4)]\times[\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau/4)]}
covers
\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}
with respect to
\Delta_{\mathrm{md}}
at scale
\tau/2
. Combined with the above estimates we get

\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{md}}(\tau)\leq\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau/4)\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau/4)\leq\big(1+32(D+1)/\tau\big)^{d+1}\big(1+16D/\tau\big)^{d}.

An upper bound of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dd}}(\tau)

By (
46
), we have
\Theta_{\mathrm{dd}}\subset\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}
, thus a similar argument yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dd}}(\tau)\leq\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{d}\times\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\Delta_{\mathrm{dd}},\tau/2)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefMetricDD}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{d},\Delta_{d},\tau/8)^{2}\mathcal{N}(\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}},\Delta_{\mathrm{mm}},\tau/4)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau/8)\mathcal{N}_{d}(\tau/8)\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{mm}}(\tau/4)

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+64(D+1)/\tau\big)^{2(d+1)}\big(1+16D/\tau\big)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+64(D+1)/\tau\big)^{3d+2}.

To conclude, observe that the last bound dominates all the previous ones.

A.8
Proof of Theorem
22

Since the average marginal density of the
\omega_{j}
’s satisfies (
22
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Psi_{\mathrm{m}}(\bm{\Omega})

\displaystyle=1

(118)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})

\displaystyle=1,\quad\forall z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{d}}

(119)

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\Psi_{\mathrm{\ell}}(z|\bm{\Omega})|

\displaystyle\leq\mu\quad\forall\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\},\ \forall z\in\Theta_{\ell}.

(120)

The proof of (
120
) ((

) and (

) are obtained similarly) is postponed to the end of this section. By (

) we have

|\Psi_{\mathrm{\ell}}(z|\bm{\Omega})|\leq|\Psi_{\mathrm{\ell}}(z|\bm{\Omega})-\mathbb{E}\Psi_{\mathrm{\ell}}(z|\bm{\Omega})|+\mu,\quad\forall\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\},\ \forall z\in\Theta_{\ell}

hence (

),(

),(

) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|\Psi_{\mathrm{m}}(\bm{\Omega})-1|>\frac{\tau}{4}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\,,

(121)

\displaystyle\forall z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{d}},\>\>\mathbb{P}\Big(|\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})-1|>\frac{\tau}{8}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\,,

(122)

\displaystyle\forall\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm,md,dd}\},\>\>\forall z\in\Theta_{\ell},\>\>\mathbb{P}\Big(|\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})|>\mu+\frac{\tau}{16k}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\,.

(123)

Now, as mentioned in
Section

2.3
(after
Definition

5
), the mutual incoherence assumption implies that
\kappa
is locally characteristic with respect to
\mathcal{T}
and that its
2k
-coherence is bounded by
c=(2k-1)\mu
. Since
\kappa\geq 0
, all assumptions of
Propositions

16
,
18
and
19
and
Theorem

20
thus hold.
For each
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d,mm,md,dd}\}
consider a covering
(z_{i}^{\ell})_{i\in[\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})]}
of
\Theta_{\ell}
with respect to
\Delta_{\ell}
at scale

\tau^{\prime}:=\frac{\tau}{96Mk\cdot C_{\kappa}}.

(124)

By
Theorem

20
, if

\displaystyle\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})

\displaystyle\leq M\,,

(125)

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{d}}(\tau^{\prime})]}|\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z_{i}^{\mathrm{d}}|\bm{\Omega})-1|

\displaystyle\leq\tau/8

(126)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm,md,dd}\}}\max_{i\in[\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})]}|\Psi_{\ell}(z_{i}^{\ell}|\bm{\Omega})|

\displaystyle\leq\mu+\frac{\tau}{16k}

(127)

then by (
50
), for each
\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm,md,dd}\}
and
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
there is
i\in[\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})]
such that

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})|\leq|\Psi_{\ell}(z_{i}^{\ell}|\bm{\Omega})|+|\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})-\Psi_{\ell}(z_{i}^{\ell}|\bm{\Omega})|

\displaystyle\leq\mu+\frac{\tau}{16k}+6M\cdot C_{\kappa}\cdot\underbrace{\Delta_{\ell}(z,z_{i}^{\ell})}_{\leq\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu+\frac{\tau}{16k}+\frac{\tau}{16k}\leq\mu+\frac{\tau}{8k}\,,

and similarly for each

there is

such that

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})-1|\leq|\Psi_{\ell}(z_{i}^{\mathrm{d}}|\bm{\Omega})-1|+|\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})-\Psi_{\ell}(z_{i}^{\mathrm{d}}|\bm{\Omega})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau}{8}+\frac{\tau}{8}\leq\frac{\tau}{4}.

Thus, when (
125
)-(
126
)-(
127
) hold we have

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{d}}}|\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})-1|\leq\frac{\tau}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm,md,dd}\}}\sup\limits_{z\in\Theta_{\ell}}|\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})|\leq\mu+\frac{\tau}{8k}.

If in addition we have

\displaystyle|\Psi_{\mathrm{m}}(\bm{\Omega})-1|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau}{4}

(128)

then by
Proposition

19
and the bound (
35
), which follows from

16
and
18
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\max\big(\delta(\mathfrak{M}|\mathcal{A}),\delta(\hat{\mathfrak{D}}|\mathcal{A})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\tau/4,

\displaystyle(2k-1)\max\big(\mu(\mathfrak{M}_{\neq}^{2}|\mathcal{A}),\mu(\hat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\neq}^{2}|\mathcal{A}),\mu(\mathfrak{M}\times\hat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\neq}|\mathcal{A}))\big)

\displaystyle\leq(2k-1)\mu+\tau/4=c+\tau/4,

\displaystyle\delta(\mathcal{S}_{k}|\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-c}\big(c+\frac{\tau}{4}+3(c+\frac{\tau}{4})\big)=\frac{4c+\tau}{1-c}.

Observe that, since
0<\tau<1-5c
, we have
4c+\tau<1-c
hence
(4c+\tau)/(1-c)<1
.

To conclude, we bound the probability
p
that one of the inequalities (
125
)-(
126
)-(
127
)-(
128
) fails to hold.
Using (
124
),
denoting
D:=\operatorname{diam}_{a}(\Theta)
, we have
1+64(D+1)/\tau^{\prime}=1+6144Mk(D+1)C_{\kappa}/\tau=1+C/\tau
.
By a union bound combining (
56
)-(
121
)-(
122
)-(
123
)
and by
Proposition

21
we obtain

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\gamma}{2}+1+\sum_{\ell\in\{\mathrm{d,mm,md,dd}\}}\mathcal{N}_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\gamma}{2}+1+4\big(1+64(D+1)/\tau^{\prime}\big)^{3d+2}\Big)\leq(\gamma+10)\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{v}\Big)\cdot\big(1+C/\tau\big)^{3d+2}.

Proof of (
120
).

Let
\ell\in\{\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\}
and let
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
. First, by
Lemma

37
, there exists
(\iota,\iota^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{D}_{\neq}^{2}
and
\xi\in\{-1,1\}

such that
\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})=\xi\mathfrak{Re}\langle\mathcal{A}\iota,\mathcal{A}\iota^{\prime}\rangle
for any choice of
\bm{\Omega}
(and of the corresponding sketching operator
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{\bm{\Omega}}
). In the following, we show that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\Omega}}\mathfrak{Re}\langle\mathcal{A}\iota,\mathcal{A}\iota^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\iota,\iota^{\prime}\rangle_{\kappa}
, so that, by the definition (
13
) of the mutual coherence with respect to
\mathcal{T}
, we get

|\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\Omega}}\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})|=|\langle\iota,\iota^{\prime}\rangle_{\kappa}|\leq\mu
. Since
\langle\iota,\iota^{\prime}\rangle_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{R}
, it is enough to prove that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\Omega}}\langle\mathcal{A}\iota,\mathcal{A}\iota^{\prime}\rangle=\langle\iota,\iota^{\prime}\rangle_{\kappa}
. Now, remember that
\langle\mathcal{A}\iota,\mathcal{A}\iota^{\prime}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\langle\phi_{\omega_{j}},\iota\rangle\overline{\langle\phi_{\omega_{j}},\iota^{\prime}\rangle}/m
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\Omega}}\langle\mathcal{A}\iota,\mathcal{A}\iota^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\Omega}}\langle\phi_{\omega_{j}},\iota\rangle\overline{\langle\phi_{\omega_{j}},\iota^{\prime}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda_{j}(\omega)\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota\rangle\overline{\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota^{\prime}\rangle}\mathrm{d}\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\Lambda_{j}(\omega)\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota\rangle\overline{\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota^{\prime}\rangle}\mathrm{d}\omega

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:ImportanceSamplingStructured}}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w(\omega)^{2}\hat{\kappa}(\omega)\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota\rangle\overline{\langle\phi_{\omega},\iota^{\prime}\rangle}\mathrm{d}\omega=\langle\iota,\iota^{\prime}\rangle_{\kappa}.

A.9
Proof of Theorem
26
and its corollaries

In this section, we prove Theorem
26
, Corollary
27
and Corollary
28
. We start by establishing
Lemma

24
, and a few lemmas to deal with sub-exponential random variables.

A.9.1
Proof of
Lemma

24

The case
\ell=\mathrm{d}
.
With
x=z\in\Theta_{d}
,
x^{\prime}:=x/\|x\|_{a}
we have
\|x^{\prime}\|_{a}=1
. As
|\sin t|\leq|t|
for all
t

\displaystyle|f_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\omega)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefPsiD}}}{{=}}2\frac{\sin^{2}(\langle\omega,x\rangle/2)}{1-\tilde{\kappa}(\|x\|_{a})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefAlphaFn}}}{{=}}2\alpha^{2}(\|x\|_{a})\frac{\sin^{2}(\langle\omega,x\rangle/2)}{\|x\|_{a}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:conditions_on_alpha}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{2}C_{\kappa}\langle\omega,x^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\leq C_{\kappa}|\langle\omega,x^{\prime}\rangle|^{2}.

This establishes (
61
) with
p=p_{\mathrm{d}}=2
.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{mm}
.

Denote
y=z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{mm}}
. We have
|f_{\mathrm{mm}}(z|\omega)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefPsiMM}}}{{=}}|\cos(\langle\omega,y\rangle)|\leq 1
.
This establishes (
61
) with
p=p_{\mathrm{mm}}=0
.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{md}
.

With
(x,y)=z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{md}}
and
x^{\prime}:=x/\|x\|_{a}
we have
\|x^{\prime}\|_{a}=1
and

\displaystyle|f_{\mathrm{md}}(z|\omega)|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefPsiMD}}}{{=}}2\Bigg|\frac{\sin\big(\langle\omega,x/2\rangle\big)\sin\big(\langle\omega,y+x/2\rangle\big)}{\sqrt{2(1-\bar{\kappa}(x))}}\Bigg|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefAlphaFn}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}\alpha(\|x\|_{a})\Bigg|\frac{\sin\big(\langle\omega,x/2\rangle\big)}{\|x\|_{a}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:conditions_on_alpha}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\bigg|\Big\langle\omega,\frac{x}{2\|x\|_{a}}\Big\rangle\bigg|\leq\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}|\langle\omega,x^{\prime}\rangle|.

This establishes (
61
) with
p=p_{\mathrm{md}}=1
.

The case
\ell=\mathrm{dd}
.

With
(x_{1},x_{2},y)=z\in\Theta_{\mathrm{dd}}
and
x^{\prime}_{i}=x_{i}/\|x_{i}\|_{a}
we have
\|x^{\prime}_{i}\|_{a}=1
and

\displaystyle|f_{\mathrm{dd}}(z|\omega)|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefPsiDD}}}{{=}}4\Bigg|\frac{\sin\big(\langle\omega,x_{1}/2\rangle\big)\sin\big(\langle\omega,x_{2}/2\rangle\big)\cos\big(\langle\omega,y+x_{2}/2-x_{1}/2\rangle\big)}{\sqrt{2(1-\bar{\kappa}(x_{1}))}\sqrt{2(1-\bar{\kappa}(x_{2}))}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:DefAlphaFn}}}{{\leq}}2\alpha(\|x_{1}\|_{a})\alpha(\|x_{2}\|_{a})\Bigg|\frac{\sin\big(\langle\omega,x_{1}/2\rangle\big)\sin\big(\langle\omega,x_{2}/2\rangle\big)}{\|x_{1}\|_{a}\|x_{2}\|_{a}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:conditions_on_alpha}}}{{\leq}}\frac{C_{\kappa}}{2}\big|\langle\omega,x_{1}/\|x_{1}\|_{a}\rangle\langle\omega,x_{2}/\|x_{2}\|_{a}\rangle\big|=\frac{C_{\kappa}}{2}\big|\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}}{4}\Big(\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle^{2}+\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle^{2}\Big).

A.9.2
Some properties of sub-exponential random variables

Proof of
Lemma

25
.

Denote
E:=2\mathbb{E}Y
. If
E=0
then
Y=X=0
almost surely, hence
X
(and
Y
) are both sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
) for any choice of
\nu^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\geq 0
so the result is trivial. Assume now that
E>0
.
Since
|X-\mathbb{E}X|\leq|X|+|\mathbb{E}X|\leq Y+\mathbb{E}Y
almost surely, we get

\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(X-\mathbb{E}X)}=\sum_{q=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\lambda^{q}}{q!}\mathbb{E}(X-\mathbb{E}X)^{q}\leq 1+\sum_{q=2}^{+\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{q}}{q!}\mathbb{E}|X-\mathbb{E}X|^{q}\leq 1+\sum_{q=2}^{+\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{q}}{q!}\mathbb{E}(Y+\mathbb{E}Y)^{q}.

Using the binomial formula this yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(X-\mathbb{E}X)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{q=2}^{+\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{q}}{q!}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{q}{q\choose j}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{j}E^{q-j}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{j}}{j!}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{j}\cdot\sum_{q\geq\max(j,2)}\frac{|\lambda|^{q-j}j!}{q!}{q\choose j}E^{q-j}.

Observe that
\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{0}=1
,
\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{1}=0
, and

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geq\max(j,2)}\frac{|\lambda|^{q-j}j!}{q!}{q\choose j}E^{q-j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\geq\max(j,2)}\frac{|\lambda|^{q-j}}{(q-j)!}E^{q-j}=\begin{cases}\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{(|\lambda|E)^{k}}{k!}=e^{|\lambda|E},&j\geq 2\\
\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{(|\lambda|E)^{k}}{k!}=e^{|\lambda|E}-1-|\lambda|E,&j=0.\end{cases}

Since
Y
is sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
), when
|\lambda|\leq 1/\beta
we obtain (using again that
\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{j}=0
for
j=1
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(X-\mathbb{E}X)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+(e^{|\lambda|E}-1-|\lambda|E)+\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{j}}{j!}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{j}e^{|\lambda|E}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{j}}{j!}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)^{j}\Big)e^{|\lambda|E}-|\lambda|E=\mathbb{E}e^{|\lambda|(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)}e^{|\lambda|E}-|\lambda|E

\displaystyle\leq e^{\nu^{2}\lambda^{2}/2+|\lambda|E}-|\lambda|E.

To conclude we use a technical lemma which proof is postponed to
Section

A.9.7
.

Lemma 38
.

For
\alpha>0
, we have

\forall t\geq 0,\>\>e^{\alpha t^{2}/2+t}-t\leq e^{(\alpha+2)t^{2}}.

Applying the lemma with
\alpha=(\nu/E)^{2}
,
t=|\lambda|E
, we obtain

|\lambda|\leq 1/\beta\ \Longrightarrow\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(X-\mathbb{E}X)}\leq e^{\frac{\nu^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}+|\lambda|E}-|\lambda|E=e^{\frac{\alpha t^{2}}{2}+t}-t\leq e^{(\alpha+2)t^{2}}=e^{\frac{2(\nu^{2}+2E^{2})\lambda^{2}}{2}}.

This shows that
X
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta
) with
(\nu^{\prime})^{2}=2(\nu^{2}+2E^{2})=2\nu^{2}+16(\mathbb{E}Y)^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

If
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
then
X^{2}
is sub-exp(
2,4
) and
|X|
is sub-exp(
4,4
).

Proof.

Since
Y:=X^{2}
follows the chi-squared distribution of one degree of freedom, by
(
Forbes et al. 2011
)
we have

\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\lambda}},\>\>\forall|\lambda|<\frac{1}{2}.

In particular, since
\mathbb{E}Y=1
, then

\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(Y-\mathbb{E}Y)}=\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(Y-1)}=\frac{e^{-\lambda}}{\sqrt{1-2\lambda}}\leq e^{\frac{2^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}},\>\>\forall|\lambda|\leq\frac{1}{4}.

This is the definition of a sub-exp(
2,4
) random variable.

Now, considering
Z:=|X|
, since
|t|\leq t^{2}+1/4
for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

Z=|X|\leq\frac{1}{4}+X^{2}.\>\>\text{a.s.}

Since
X^{2}
is sub-exp(
2,4
),
X^{2}+1/4
is also sub-exp(
2,4
). As
\mathbb{E}(X^{2}+1/4)=5/4\leq\sqrt{2}
, by
Lemma

25
,
X
is sub-exp(
\nu,4
), with

\nu^{2}:=2\times 2^{2}+4(5/4)^{2}\leq 16
, hence
Z
is sub-exp(
4,4
).

∎

Lemma 40
.

Consider
X_{i}
,
i=1,2
two real-valued random variables (possibly non independent), assumed to be respectively sub-exp(
\nu_{i},\beta_{i}
). Then
X_{1}+X_{2}
is sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
) where

\nu:=\nu_{1}+\nu_{2},\>\>\text{and}\>\>\beta:=\max\Big(\frac{\beta_{1}(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})}{\nu_{1}},\frac{\beta_{2}(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})}{\nu_{2}}\Big).

Proof.

Let
p=(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/\nu_{1}
and
q=(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/\nu_{2}
, so that
1/p+1/q=1
. By Hölder’s inequality and
Definition

23
, if
|\lambda|\leq\min(\frac{1}{p\beta_{1}},\frac{1}{q\beta_{2}})=\min\Big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\beta_{1}(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})},\frac{\nu_{2}}{\beta_{2}(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})}\Big)
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(X_{1}+X_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda pX_{1}})^{1/p}(\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda qX_{2}})^{1/q}\leq e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}p^{2}\nu_{1}^{2}}{2p}}e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}q^{2}\nu_{2}^{2}}{2q}}\leq e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}(p\nu_{1}^{2}+q\nu_{2}^{2})}{2}}=e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})^{2}}{2}}.

∎

A.9.3
Proof of
Theorem

26

The proof of
Theorem

26
leverages
Theorem

22
.
Before exploiting this theorem we check that the basic required assumptions are met:
i)
\mathcal{T}=(\Theta,\rho,\mathcal{I})
,
\kappa\geq 0
and
\|\cdot\|_{a}
are satisfying the assumptions required in
Theorem

20
,
ii)
\kappa
is assumed to have its mutual coherence with respect to
\mathcal{T}
bounded by
0<\mu<1/10
, and
k
satisfies
1\leq k<\frac{1}{10\mu}
,
iii)
w
is a
\kappa
-compatible weight function and the average marginal density of the
\omega_{j}
’s satisfies (
22
),
iv) the assumption (
56
) holds.

Now, we move to check the more technical assumptions: (
57
),(
58
),(
59
) with
v
defined in (
64
).
For this purpose, we prove that
\Psi_{m}(\bm{\Omega})
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime}/\sqrt{m/b},\beta^{\prime}/(m/b)
) and for
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\}
, and for
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
,
\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime}\sqrt{m/b},\beta^{\prime}/(m/b)
), where we recall that
b
is the block size, and
\nu^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
will be specified in due time to derive (
57
),(
58
),(
59
) using (
60
).

First, consider
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\}
and
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
, and observe that

\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})=\frac{1}{m/b}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m/b}\underbrace{\frac{1}{b}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{(j-1)b+i})f_{\ell}(z|\omega_{(j-1)b+i})}_{=:X_{j}},

where by assumption the random variables
X_{j}
are independent and identically distributed.
A well-known property of sub-exp random variables is that if
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are independent sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
) then
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{j}
is sub-exp(
\nu/\sqrt{n},\beta/n
). Thus, in order to prove that
\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime}/\sqrt{m/b},\beta^{\prime}/(m/b)
), it is enough to prove that the random variable

X:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})f_{\ell}(z|\omega_{i})

is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
).
For this purpose, we make use of
Lemmas

24
and
25
.

Consider arbitrary
x^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
t\in\{0,1,2\}
, s.t.
\|x^{\prime}_{t}\|_{a}=1
, and for
t,p\in\{0,1,2\}
denote,
Z_{t,p}:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})(\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}|\langle\omega_{i},x^{\prime}_{t}\rangle|)^{p}
. By assumption, each
Z_{t,p}

is sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
) with
|\mathbb{E}(Z_{t,p})|\leq B
.
We distinguish two cases

•

if
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md}\}
, since
\psi(.)\geq 0
, the first claim of
Lemma

24

implies that
|X|\leq Z_{0,p}
for some choice of
x^{\prime}_{0}
, hence by
Lemma

25

X
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta
),
where

\nu^{\prime}:=\sqrt{2\nu^{2}+16B^{2}}.

(129)

•

if
\ell=\mathrm{dd}
by the second claim of
Lemma

24
we similarly get the existence of
x^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying
\|x^{\prime}_{t}\|_{a}=1
,
t=1,2
, such that

|X|\leq Z^{\prime}
with
Z^{\prime}:=(Z_{1,2}+Z_{2,2})/4
.
By Lemma
40
,
Z_{1,2}+Z_{2,2}
is sub-exp(
2\nu,2\beta
), and
|\mathbb{E}(Z_{1,2}+Z_{2,2})|\leq 2B
, hence
Z^{\prime}:=(Z_{1,2}+Z_{2,2})/4

is also sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
) with
|\mathbb{E}(Z^{\prime})|\leq B
. By
Lemma

25
we also get that
X
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta
) with
\nu^{\prime}
as in (
129
).

Now, consider
\ell=\mathrm{m}
. Similarly, to prove that
\Psi_{m}(\bm{\Omega})
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime}/\sqrt{m/b},\beta^{\prime}/(m/b)
), it is enough to prove that
X:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})
is sub-exp(
\nu^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
). Since
\psi(\omega)=Z_{0,0}
for any choice of
x^{\prime}_{0}
, this is indeed true with
\nu^{\prime}
as in (
129
) and
\beta^{\prime}=\beta
.

Now, we use (
60
) applied to
\Psi_{\mathrm{m}}(\bm{\Omega})
with
t=\frac{\tau}{4}
, and to
\Psi_{\mathrm{d}}(z|\bm{\Omega})
with
t=\frac{\tau}{8}
, and to
\Psi_{\ell}(z|\bm{\Omega})
with
t=\frac{\tau}{16k}
for
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md},\mathrm{dd}\}
and
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
, to get
that the left hand side of (
57
),(
58
),(
59
) is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\max_{t\in\{\frac{\tau}{4},\frac{\tau}{8},\frac{\tau}{16k}\}}2\exp\left(-\frac{(m/b)t^{2}}{2\nu^{\prime 2}+\beta t}\right)=2\exp\left(-\frac{(m/b)\left(\frac{\tau}{16k}\right)^{2}}{2\nu^{\prime 2}+\beta\left(\frac{\tau}{16k}\right)}\right)=2\exp\left(-\frac{m\tau^{2}}{256bk^{2}(2\nu^{\prime 2}+\beta\left(\frac{\tau}{16k}\right)}\right)

where we used that
t\mapsto t^{2}/(2(\nu^{\prime})^{2}+\beta t)
is an increasing function.
We conclude by observing that, with
v
as defined in (
64
), we have

256bk^{2}(2\nu^{\prime 2}+\beta\frac{\tau}{16k})\leq 256bk^{2}(2\nu^{\prime 2}+\beta\tau)=\tau^{2}v,

To prove the variant of the theorem, we first reason as above to show that the modified assumptions imply that
for arbitrary
x^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
t\in\{0,1,2\}
, s.t.
\|x^{\prime}_{t}\|_{a}=1
the random variable

Y_{p}:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}B_{\psi}(\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}|\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle|)^{p}
is sub-exp(
B_{\psi}\nu,B_{\psi}\beta
) with
|\mathbb{E}Y_{p}|\leq B_{\psi}\beta
, and similarly for
Y^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}B_{\psi}C_{\kappa}(\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle^{2}+\langle\omega,x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle^{2})/4
. Then, using the definition of
B_{\psi}
we obtain that:
for
\ell=\mathrm{m}
,
X:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})
satisfies
|X|\leq Y_{0}
;
for
\ell\in\{\mathrm{d},\mathrm{mm},\mathrm{md}\}
and
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
,

X:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})f_{\ell}(z|\omega_{i})
satisfies
|X|\leq Y_{p}
for an appropriate choice of
x^{\prime}_{0}
,
p\in\{0,1,2\}
;
for
\ell=\mathrm{dd}
and
z\in\Theta_{\ell}
,

X:=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\psi(\omega_{i})f_{\ell}(z|\omega_{i})
satisfies
|X|\leq Y^{\prime}
for an appropriate choice of
x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2}
. The same reasoning as above yields that
X
is sub-exp(
B_{\psi}\nu^{\prime},B_{\psi}\beta
) with
\nu^{\prime}
as in (
129
). We conclude similarly once we observe that
256bk^{2}(2B_{\psi}^{2}\nu^{\prime 2}+B_{\psi}\beta\tau)=\tau^{2}v^{\prime}
.

A.9.4
Proofs of Corollary
27
and Corollary
28

Corollary

27
and
Corollary

28
are direct consequences of
Theorem

26
.
To see why, we check that the assumptions of these theorems are met in these settings.

Checking that the assumptions on
\mathcal{T}
and
\kappa
hold, and controlling
C_{\kappa}
,
\mu
.

First, observe that, in the setting of Gaussian (resp. Dirac) mixtures of
Example

8
(resp. of
Example

7
), the kernel satisfies
\kappa\geq 0
, and (
47
) holds with
\|\cdot\|_{a}=\|\cdot\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}
(resp. with
\|\cdot\|_{a}=\|\cdot\|_{2}
) and
\tilde{\kappa}(r)=e^{-r^{2}/\sigma^{2}}

with
\sigma:=\sqrt{2(2+s^{2})}
(resp. with
\sigma:=\sqrt{2}s
). In both settings we have
\varrho=\|\cdot\|_{a}/\epsilon
,
and by the definition of
\Theta_{\mathrm{d}}
(see (
43
), with
\|\cdot\|:=\|\cdot\|_{a}/\epsilon
), we have
R:=\sup_{x\in\Theta_{\mathrm{d}}}\|x\|_{a}\leq\epsilon
.
Thus, by Lemma
41
, the constant
C_{\kappa}
from (
49
) satisfies

C_{\kappa}\leq\max\big(1,\sqrt[4]{3}R,\sqrt[4]{3}\sigma\big)^{2}\leq\max\big(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2},\sqrt{3}\sigma^{2}\big).

Now we proceed separately for the two settings.

•

For Gaussian mixtures, as
\sqrt{2+s^{2}}\leq\epsilon(4\sqrt{\log(ek)})^{-1}
, we get
\sigma=\sqrt{2(2+s^{2})}\leq\epsilon
, and
by
(
Gribonval et al. 2021a
, Theorem 5.16, Lemma 6.10)

\kappa
is locally characteristic with mutual coherence with respect to
\mathcal{T}
bounded by
\mu\leq 12/(16(10k-1))
.

•

For mixtures of Diracs, since
s\leq\epsilon(4\sqrt{\log(5ek)})^{-1}
, we get
\sigma:=\sqrt{2}s\leq\epsilon
and the bound on the coherence holds by
(
Gribonval et al. 2021a
, Theorem 5.16, Lemma 6.10)
.

In both cases, we get
\mu\leq 12/(16(10k-1))m\leq 1/(10k)
and
\sigma\leq\epsilon
. The latter implies

C_{\kappa}\leq\max\big(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2},\sqrt{3}\sigma^{2}\big)=\max(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}).

(130)

Since
B_{\psi}:=\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi(\omega)
is finite in both settings, we may use the
variant of
Theorem

26
. Indeed, by (
69
) (resp. by (
74
))
B_{\psi}=(1+2s^{-2})^{d/2}
for Gaussian mixtures (resp.
B_{\psi}=1
for mixtures of Diracs). Now, we check that the assumptions expressed in
Equation

62
(in its variant
involving
Z^{\prime}_{p}
) and
Equation

64
of
Theorem

26

hold in both settings.

Checking the variant of
Equation

62
in
Theorem

26

We show the existence of
\nu,\beta,B>0
such that the random variables
Z^{\prime}_{p}
defined in (
68
) are sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
) with
|\mathbb{E}(Z_{p})|\leq B
. Since the frequencies
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{m}
are i.i.d., we consider a block size
b=1
and

Z^{\prime}_{p}=(\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}|\langle\omega,x\rangle|)^{p}

with
\omega\sim\Lambda
.
We will indeed prove that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
s.t.
\|x\|_{a}=1
, the random variables
|\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\langle\omega,x\rangle|^{p}
,
p\in\{0,1,2\}
are sub-exp
(\nu,\beta)
with
|\mathbb{E}Z_{p}|\leq B
,
where

B:=\max(1,C_{\kappa}s^{-2}),\quad\textit{and}\quad\nu=\beta=4B.

(131)

This is done in the following. To handle both settings in a common framework, define
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{d}
for the setting of a mixture of Diracs, so that in both cases we have
\|\cdot\|_{a}=\|\cdot\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}
.
Thus, a vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies
\|x\|_{a}=\|x\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}=1
if, and only if,
\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}x\|_{2}=1
.
Since
\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,s^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1})
we have
s\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{d})
hence
s\langle\omega,x\rangle=s\langle\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\omega,\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}x\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, so that
(\mathbb{E}|\langle\omega,x\rangle|)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}|\langle\omega,x\rangle|^{2}=1/s^{2}
, hence using also that
|\langle\omega,x\rangle|^{0}\equiv 1
we obtain

\max_{p\in\{0,1,2\}}\mathbb{E}|\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\langle\omega,x\rangle|^{p}\leq\max(1,\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}s^{-1},C_{\kappa}s^{-2})=B.

By Lemma
39
,
s|\langle\omega,x\rangle|
is sub-exp(
4,4
) and
s^{2}\langle\omega,x\rangle^{2}
is sub-exp(
2,4
), hence
|\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\langle\omega,x\rangle|
is sub-exp(
4\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}/s,4\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}/s
), and
C_{\kappa}\langle\omega,x\rangle^{2}
is sub-exp(
2C_{\kappa}/s^{2},4C_{\kappa}/s^{2}
). Observe that
\max(4\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}/s,2C_{\kappa}/s^{2})\leq 4B
and
\max(4\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}/s,4C_{\kappa}/s^{2})\leq 4B
to conclude that
|\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\langle\omega,x\rangle|
and
C_{\kappa}\langle\omega,x\rangle^{2}
are indeed both sub-exp(
\nu,b
) with
\nu=b=4B
.
The same holds for
p=0
since
|\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}\langle\omega,x\rangle|^{0}\equiv 1
.

NB: in the setting of Gaussian mixtures, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\|x\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}=1
,
\psi(\omega)
and
\psi(\omega)|\langle\omega,x\rangle|
and
\psi(\omega)\langle\omega,x\rangle^{2}
are bounded and we may alternatively have used Hoeffding’s inequality
(
Hoeffding 1994
)
instead of Theorem
26
.
We chose to use the latter as it allows to encompass both settings under the same reasoning.

We move now to check that
Equation

64
holds in both settings.

Identifying
M
such that
Equation

64
in
Theorem

26
holds with
\gamma=1
for Gaussian mixtures with any
v>0
.

We show that

|\psi(\omega)f_{0}(\omega)|\leq M:=4B_{\psi},\ \forall\omega,

(132)

hence (
63
) holds
for any
v>0
. In particular, (
63
) holds for
v=v_{k}(\tau)
, with
v_{k}(\tau)
defined in (
72
). Indeed, for every
t\geq 0
we have
t^{2}\leq(t+t^{3})/2
(since
t(t-1)^{2}\geq 0
), hence given the definition (
51
) of
f_{0}
and since
\|\cdot\|_{a,\star}=\|\cdot\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}
we have
f_{0}(\omega)\leq\frac{3}{2}(\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}+\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}^{3})
for every
\omega
, so that

\displaystyle|\psi(\omega)f_{0}(\omega)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:psi_expression_gaussian}}}{{=}}B_{\psi}e^{-\omega^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\Sigma}\omega/2}f_{0}(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}B_{\psi}e^{-\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}^{2}/2}(\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}+\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}^{3})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}B_{\psi}\varphi(\|\omega\|_{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}})\leq 4B_{\psi}

since
\varphi(t):=(t+t^{3})e^{-t^{2}/2}
satisfies
\varphi(t)\leq 8/3
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Indeed, we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\varphi(t)\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{1}(t)+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{2}(t),

where
\varphi_{1}(t):=te^{-t^{2}/2}
, and
\varphi_{2}(t):=t^{3}e^{-t^{2}/2}
, and it is easy to prove that
\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{1}(t)=\varphi_{1}(1)=e^{-1/2}\leq 1
,
and
\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{2}(t)=\varphi_{2}(\sqrt{3})=3\sqrt{3}e^{-3/2}\approx 1.16\leq 5/3\approx 1.66
.

Identifying
M
such that
Equation

64
holds with
\gamma=1
for mixtures of Diracs with
v=v_{k}(\tau)
.

Since
\psi(\omega)=1
(cf (
74
)) we have
\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}f_{0}(\omega_{j})
, with
f_{0}(\omega)
defined in (
51
). Since
\|\cdot\|_{a}=\|\cdot\|_{2}
we have
\|\cdot\|_{a,\star}=\|\cdot\|_{2}
hence
f_{0}(\omega)=\sum_{t=1}^{3}\|\omega\|_{2}^{t}
. As
\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,s^{-2}\mathbb{I}_{d})
,
Proposition

42
yields

\displaystyle\forall t\geq 0,\quad\mathbb{P}\Big(\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})>\tilde{M}(t)\Big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{(mt)^{2/3}}{2}\bigg),

with
\tilde{M}(t):=1+\frac{8}{s^{3}}\big(\sqrt{\frac{8}{\pi}}d^{3/2}+t\big)
.
We define

C_{0}:=7B_{\psi}B

(133)

and recall that
C_{0}\geq 7
since
B_{\psi}\geq 1
and
B\geq 1
.
Therefore, for
0<\tau\leq 1
, and
v_{k}(\tau)
as defined in (
77
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})>\tilde{M}(\tau^{3/2}m^{1/2})\Big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{m\tau}{2}\bigg)\leq\exp(-m/v_{k}(\tau)),

since
2/\tau\leq 2/\tau^{2}\leq 2(C_{0}/\tau)^{2}\leq v_{k}(\tau)
.

In other words, (
63
) holds with
M=\tilde{M}(\tau^{3/2}m^{1/2})
and
v=v_{k}(\tau)
.

Wrapping up the proof.

To complete the proof of
Corollary

27
and
Corollary

28
we use Theorem
26
and the last step is to give explicit upper bounds of the constants
C
and
v^{\prime}
respectively defined in (
66
) and (
67
).

We start with
v^{\prime}
, and we show that it is upper bounded by
v_{k}(\tau)
defined in (
72
) (resp. in (
77
)). By (
131
),
\nu=\beta=4B
hence, by (
64
)
\nu^{\prime}=\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\nu^{2}+8B^{2}}=\sqrt{48B^{2}}\leq\sqrt{49B^{2}}=7B
. Hence,
B_{\psi}^{2}\nu^{\prime 2}\leq(7B_{\psi}B)^{2}=C_{0}^{2}
and
B_{\psi}\beta=4B_{\psi}B=\frac{4}{7}C_{0}
where
C_{0}
is defined in (
133
). Since the block size is
b=1
, by (
67
) we obtain

\displaystyle v^{\prime}=\frac{256k^{2}b(2B_{\psi}^{2}\nu^{\prime 2}+B_{\psi}\beta\tau)}{\tau^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 512k^{2}\left((C_{0}/\tau)^{2}+\frac{2}{7}(C_{0}/\tau)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 512k^{2}\left((C_{0}/\tau)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}(C_{0}/\tau)\right).

This matches the expressions of
v_{k}(\tau)
used in (
72
) and (
77
). Soon we will also prove that
C_{0}
satisfies the bounds expressed in (
72
) and (
77
).

As for
C
, observe that by definition (
66
),

C=6144MC_{\kappa}\cdot k(1+\mathrm{diam}_{a}(\Theta))
, so that we only need to give an upper bound of
6144MC_{\kappa}
to get the expressions that appear in (
73
) and (
78
).

To conclude we bound
C_{0}
and
6144MC_{\kappa}
. First, by (
130
), we have
C_{\kappa}\leq\max(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2})
, and by (
131
)
B=\max(1,C_{\kappa}s^{-2})
. We study separately the two settings:

•

For mixtures of Gaussians, by (
70
) we have
\epsilon\geq\max(s,1)
hence
C_{\kappa}\leq\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}
,
B\leq\sqrt{3}(\epsilon/s)^{2}
. Since
B_{\psi}=(1+2s^{-2})^{d/2}
,
we get by (
133
)

C_{0}=7BB_{\psi}\leq 7\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}s^{-2}(1+2s^{-2})^{d/2}
as claimed in (
72
). Since
M=4B_{\psi}
by (
132
), we get
6144MC_{\kappa}\leq(4\times 6144\sqrt{3})\epsilon^{2}B_{\psi}\leq 43000\epsilon^{2}B_{\psi}
as claimed in (
73
).

•

For mixtures of Diracs, by (
75
) we have
\epsilon\geq s
hence

\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}s^{-2}\geq 1
. As
C_{\kappa}s^{-2}\leq\max(s^{-2},\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}s^{-2})
, it follows that
B=\max(1,C_{\kappa}s^{-2})\leq\max(1,s^{-2},\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}s^{-2})=\max(s^{-2},\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2}s^{-2})=s^{-2}\max(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2})
. Since
B_{\psi}=1
it follows by (
133
) that

C_{0}=7BB_{\psi}=7B\leq 7s^{-2}\max(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2})
as claimed in (
77
).
Finally observe that
c:=\sqrt{8/\pi}\approx 1.6
so that
cd^{3/2}+t\geq 1
for every
t>0
hence
\tilde{M}(t)=1+\frac{8}{s^{3}}(cd^{3/2}+t)\leq(1+8/s^{3})(cd^{3/2}+t)\leq(1+2/s)^{3}(cd^{3/2}+t)\leq(1+2/s)^{3}(2d^{3/2}+t)
.
Since
M=\tilde{M}(m^{1/2}\tau^{3/2})
it follows that

6144MC_{\kappa}\leq 6144(1+2/s)^{3}\max(1,\sqrt{3}\epsilon^{2})(2d^{3/2}+\sqrt{m}\tau^{3/2})
as claimed in (
78
).

A.9.5
Proof of
Corollary

29

The proof follows the same steps as the proofs of
Corollary

27
and
Corollary

28
given in
Section

A.9.4
, even though the
\omega_{j}
are not independent, we can still prove that
Equation

62
and
Equation

64
of
Theorem

26
hold.

Indeed, by
Lemma

40
, the random variable
d\times Z_{p}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\psi(\omega_{j})(\sqrt{C_{\kappa}}|\langle\omega_{j},x\rangle|)^{p}
is sub-exp(
d\nu,d\beta
), since it is a sum of
d
random variables that are sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
), thus
Z_{p}
(and similarly
Z^{\prime}_{p}
) is sub-exp(
\nu,\beta
), and
Equation

62
holds.

As for
Equation

64
, we distinguish the two cases:

•

For mixtures of Gaussians, the variables
\psi(\omega_{j})f_{0}(\omega_{j})
are bounded and the same reasoning
as in the proof of
Corollary

27
establishes (
63
) with the same constant
M
and any
v>0
. Gathering all of the above shows that for Gaussian mixture models, sketching with the considered structured random Fourier features satisfies the RIP: (
65
)
holds with
\gamma=1
,
v=dv_{k}(\tau)
, where
v_{k}(\tau)
given in (
72
).

•

For mixtures of Diracs, we decompose
\bm{\Omega}
into
d
(non-adjacent) blocks of
m/d
i.i.d. random variables,

\bm{\Omega}_{i}:=\{\omega_{i},\omega_{i+d},\ldots,\omega_{i+(m/d-1)d}\}
,
i\in[d]
and write

\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})=\frac{1}{d}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{m/d}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m/d-1}\psi(\omega_{i+d(j-1)})f_{0}(\omega_{i+d(j-1)})=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega}_{i}),

and observe that, since the frequencies are block-i.i.d., the columns of each
\bm{\Omega}_{i}
are independent so that we can use
Proposition

42
to obtain with the same reasoning as in the proof of
Corollary

28

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega}_{i})>M\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{m/d}{v_{k}(\tau)}\Big),

where
M:=\tilde{M}(\tau^{3/2}(m/d)^{1/2})
.
A union bound yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega})>M\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\cup_{i=1}^{d}\{\Psi_{0}(\bm{\Omega}_{i})>M\}\Big)\leq d\exp\Big(-\frac{m}{dv_{k}(\tau)}\Big),

and
Equation

64
holds with
\gamma=d
,
v=dv_{k}(\tau)
and
M:=\tilde{M}(\tau^{3/2}(m/d)^{1/2})
.

A.9.6
Some helpful results

Lemma 41
.

Consider
\sigma>0
and for
r\geq 0
define
\alpha_{\sigma}(r):=\frac{r}{\sqrt{1-e^{-r^{2}/\sigma^{2}}}}
.
For each
R>0
we have

\sup\limits_{r\in[0,R]}|\alpha_{\sigma}(r)|\leq\sqrt[4]{3}\max(\sigma,R),\quad\text{and}\quad\sup\limits_{r\in(0,R]}|\alpha^{\prime}_{\sigma}(r)|\leq 1.

Proof.

First we show that
\alpha_{\sigma}(r)=\sigma\alpha_{1}(r/\sigma)\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\max(\sigma,r)
when
r\geq 0
. This implies the first bound as
(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\approx 1.26\leq 1.316\approx\sqrt[4]{3}
. When
\sigma\leq r
we have

|\alpha_{\sigma}(r)|=\frac{r}{\sqrt{1-e^{-r^{2}/\sigma^{2}}}}\leq\frac{r}{\sqrt{1-e^{-1}}}\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}r=(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\max(\sigma,r).

When
0\leq r\leq\sigma
, we prove below that
|\alpha_{1}(t)|\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}
for every
t\in[0,1]
, so that

|\alpha_{\sigma}(r)|=\sigma|\alpha_{1}(r/\sigma)|\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\sigma=(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\max(\sigma,r).

To show that
|\alpha_{1}(t)|\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}
on
[0,1]
observe that since
u\mapsto e^{-u}
is convex, it has non-decreasing slopes so that
(e^{-u}-1)/u\leq(e^{-1}-1)/1
,
\forall u\in[0,1]
. This reads
u/(1-e^{-u})\leq 1/(1-e^{-1})
and implies
|\alpha_{1}(t)|^{2}=t^{2}/(1-e^{-t^{2}})\leq 1/(1-e^{-1})
for
t\in[0,1]
, thus
|\alpha_{1}(t)|\leq 1/\sqrt{1-e^{-1}}
.

We now prove that
|\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)|\leq 1
,
\forall t>0
. This implies the second bound since
\alpha_{\sigma}^{\prime}(r)=\alpha_{1}^{\prime}(r/\sigma)
. Writing
\alpha_{1}(t)=t[v(t)]^{-1/2}

with
v(t):=1-e^{-t^{2}}
we get

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=[v(t)]^{-1/2}-\frac{t}{2}v^{\prime}(t)[v(t)]^{-3/2}=[v(t)]^{-3/2}\left(v(t)-tv^{\prime}(t)/2\right)=[v(t)]^{-3/2}\left(1-e^{-t^{2}}(1+t^{2})\right)

For each
t\geq 0
, since
e^{t^{2}}\geq 1+t^{2}
, it follows that
|\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)|=\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)
.
When
t\geq 1
, since
0<x:=e^{-t^{2}}\leq 1/e\approx 0.368<1/2<0.618\approx(\sqrt{5}-1)/2
we have

(x-1)^{3}+(1-2x)^{2}=x^{3}+x^{2}-x=x(x^{2}+x-1)=x[x+(1+\sqrt{5})/2][x+(1-\sqrt{5})/2]\leq 0
hence
(1-2x)^{2}\leq(1-x)^{3}
, and since
1-2x>0
it follows that
1-2x\leq(1-x)^{3/2}
. Thus, we obtain

|\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)|=\frac{1-e^{-t^{2}}(1+t^{2})}{\sqrt{1-e^{-t^{2}}}^{3}}\leq\frac{1-2e^{-t^{2}}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-t^{2}}}^{3}}=\frac{1-2x}{(1-x)^{3/2}}\leq 1,

Now, when
0<t\leq 1
, since
[v(t)]^{-3/2}=(\alpha_{1}(t)/t)^{3}
,
using that
|\alpha_{1}(t)|\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}\max(1,t)=(1-e^{-1})^{-1/2}
and
(1-e^{-1})^{-3/2}\approx 1.99\leq 2
we get

|\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(t)|\leq(1-e^{-1})^{-3/2}\frac{1-e^{-t^{2}}(1+t^{2})}{t^{3}}\leq 2\frac{1-e^{-t^{2}}(1+t^{2})}{t^{3}}.

It is enough to show that
g(t):=(1-e^{-t^{2}}(1+t^{2}))/t^{3}\leq 1/2
,
\forall t\in(0,1]
. We have
g^{\prime}(t)=t^{-4}(-3+e^{-t^{2}}(3+3t^{2}+2t^{4}))
hence
\mathtt{sign}(g^{\prime}(t))=-\mathtt{sign}(e^{t^{2}}-(1+t^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t^{4}))
. Thus there is a neighborhood of zero in which
g
is increasing, since
\mathtt{sign}(g^{\prime}(t))=-\mathtt{sign}(1+t^{2}+\frac{1}{2}t^{4}+O(t^{6})-(1+t^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t^{4}))=-\mathtt{sign}(-\frac{1}{6}t^{4}+O(t^{6}))=+1
for
t
small enough.
Since
g
is continuously differentiable, its supremum on
(0,1]
is thus either equal to
g(1)=1-2/e\approx 0.264<1/2
or to
g(t_{*})
, for some local maximum
0<t_{*}\leq 1
which must satisfy
g^{\prime}(t_{*})=0
. To conclude without further characterizing the existence or value of such a root, we establish that any such root must satisfy
g(t_{*})\leq 1/2
. Indeed using that
g^{\prime}(t_{*})=0
if, and only if
e^{t_{*}^{2}}=1+t_{*}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t_{*}^{4}
, we obtain

g(t_{*})=\frac{1-e^{-t_{*}^{2}}(1+t_{*}^{2})}{t_{*}^{3}}=\frac{e^{t_{*}^{2}}-(1+t_{*}^{2})}{e^{t_{*}^{2}}t_{*}^{3}}=\frac{1+t_{*}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t_{*}^{4}-(1+t_{*}^{2})}{(1+t_{*}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t_{*}^{4})t_{*}^{3}}=\frac{\frac{2}{3}t_{*}}{1+t_{*}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}t_{*}^{4}}.

We finally distinguish two cases:
i) if
t_{*}<1/\sqrt{2}
then
g(t_{*})\leq\frac{2}{3}t_{*}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}\leq 1/2
;
ii) if
1/\sqrt{2}\leq t_{*}\leq 1
then
g(t_{*})\leq\frac{\frac{2}{3}}{1+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}(1/2)^{2}}=0.4<1/2
.
∎

Proposition 42
.

Consider
s>0
, and let
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{m}
be i.i.d. samples from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{s^{2}}\mathbb{I}_{d})
. Then

\forall\tau\geq 0,\>\>\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|\omega_{j}\|^{3}>\frac{4}{s^{3}}\big(d^{3/2}\sqrt{8/\pi}+\tau\big)\Big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{(m\tau)^{2/3}}{2}\bigg).

(134)

Moreover, as a consequence we have for
\tau>0

\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{3}\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}^{t}>1+\frac{8}{s^{3}}\big(d^{3/2}\sqrt{8/\pi}+\tau\big)\Big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{(m\tau)^{2/3}}{2}\bigg).

(135)

Proof.

First, to prove (
134
) when
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{m}
are i.i.d. samples from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{s^{2}}\mathbb{I}_{d})
, it is enough to deal with the case
s=1
. Next, for
s=1
, denoting
\Omega\in\mathbb{R}^{dm}
the concatenation of
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the vector
\Omega
has independent standard normal random entries when
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{m}
are i.i.d. samples from
\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{d})
, and for any
2\leq p<\infty
the function
f_{p}:\mathbb{R}^{dm}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined by

f_{p}(\Omega):=(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}^{p})^{1/p}

is (as we will soon show)
1
-Lipschitz with respect to the Euclidean norm in
\mathbb{R}^{dm}
. Therefore, we may use the Tsirelson-Ibragimov-Sudakov inequality
(
Tsirelson et al. 1976
)
, a.k.a. concentration of a random variable that writes as a Lipschitz function of a Gaussian vector
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 5.6)
, to obtain
\mathbb{P}(f_{p}(\Omega)-\mathbb{E}f_{p}(\Omega)\geq t)\leq\exp(-t^{2}/2)
, for each
t\geq 0
, or equivalently

\forall t\geq 0,\>\>\mathbb{P}\Big(\big[f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}\geq\big[\mathbb{E}f_{p}(\Omega)+\tau\big]^{p}\Big)\leq\exp\big(-t^{2}/2\big).

By convexity we have
(a+b)^{p}=2^{p}(a/2+b/2)^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}(a^{p}+b^{p})
for every
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, hence

\forall t\geq 0,\>\>\mathbb{P}\Big(\big[f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}\geq 2^{p-1}\big(\big[\mathbb{E}f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}+t^{p}\big)\Big)\leq\exp\big(-t^{2}/2\big).

We now show that
\big[\mathbb{E}f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}\leq md^{p/2}\mathbb{E}|g|^{p}
. The convexity of
t\mapsto t^{p/2}
(
p\geq 2
) on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\omega\|_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle=d^{p/2}\mathbb{E}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}\omega_{i}^{2}\Big)^{p/2}\leq d^{p/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{d}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}|\omega_{i}|^{p}\right]=d^{p/2}\mathbb{E}|g|^{p}

where
\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{d}),\ g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. B convexity of
t\mapsto t^{p}
and Jensen’s inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\big[\mathbb{E}f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}^{p}=m\mathbb{E}\|\omega\|_{2}^{p}\leq md^{p/2}\mathbb{E}|g|^{p}.

As a result

\forall t\geq 0,\>\>\mathbb{P}(\big[f_{p}(\Omega)\big]^{p}\geq 2^{p-1}(md^{p/2}\mathbb{E}|g|^{p}+t^{p}))\leq\exp(-t^{2}/2),

or equivalently
\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\omega_{j}\|^{p}\geq 2^{p-1}\big(d^{p/2}\mathbb{E}|g|^{p}+\tau\big)\Big)\leq\exp(-(m\tau)^{2/p}/2)
for each
\tau\geq 0
.
Since
\mathbb{E}|g|^{3}=\sqrt{8/\pi}

(
Forbes et al. 2011
, Chapter 11)
, considering
p=3
yields (
134
) (for
s=1
) as claimed.
Now, observe that
for
t\geq 0
we have
t^{3}-t-(t^{2}-1)=(t^{2}-1)(t-1)=(t-1)^{2}(t+1)\geq 0
hence
t^{3}+1\geq t^{2}+t
and
t+t^{2}+t^{3}\leq 1+2t^{3}
. Thus
\sum_{i=1}^{3}\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}^{i}\leq 1+2\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}^{3}
, and we deduce
(
135
) from (
134
).

To complete the proof, we show that
f_{p}
is
1
-Lipschitz with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{2}
. Denoting
v_{\Omega}:=(\|\omega_{j}\|_{2})_{j\in[m]}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, observe that
f_{p}(\Omega)=\|v_{\Omega}\|_{p}
. Thus, for
\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{dm}
, since
p\geq 2
, we have

\big|f_{p}(\Omega)-f_{p}(\Omega^{\prime})\big|=\big|\|v_{\Omega}\|_{p}-\|v_{\Omega^{\prime}}\|_{p}\big|\leq\big\|v_{\Omega}-v_{\Omega^{\prime}}\big\|_{p}\leq\big\|v_{\Omega}-v_{\Omega^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}.

Finally,

\big\|v_{\Omega}-v_{\Omega^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}(\|\omega_{j}\|_{2}-\|\omega^{\prime}_{j}\|_{2})^{2}\leq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|\omega_{j}-\omega^{\prime}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}.

∎

A.9.7
Proof of
Lemma

38

Denote
c:=2(\alpha+2)
and
\varphi(t):=e^{-(c-\alpha)t^{2}/2+t}-te^{-ct^{2}/2}
.
Since
\varphi(0)=1
, it is enough to prove that
\varphi
is non-increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Since
\varphi
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
, we study the sign of

\varphi^{\prime}(t)=\Big(-(c-\alpha)t+1+(ct^{2}-1)e^{-\alpha t^{2}/2-t}\Big)e^{-(c-\alpha)t^{2}/2+t}

which is the sign of

\psi(t):=1-(c-\alpha)t+(ct^{2}-1)e^{-\alpha t^{2}/2-t}.

To show that
\psi(t)\leq 0
for each
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
we study its sign on the intervals
(0,\frac{1}{c-\alpha})
and
(\frac{1}{c-\alpha},+\infty)
. As a preliminary we record that since
\alpha>0
, we have

\sqrt{c}/(c-\alpha)=\sqrt{2(2+\alpha)}/(\alpha+4)\leq 1/2.

(136)

Case of
t\in(0,\frac{1}{c-\alpha})
.

Since
1-(c-\alpha)t>0
, we will get that
\psi(t)\leq 0
if we show that

\frac{1-ct^{2}}{1-(c-\alpha)t}\geq e^{\alpha t^{2}/2+t}.

Since
t\in(0,1/(c-\alpha))
, using (
136
) we have
\sqrt{c}t<(c-\alpha)t<1
hence

\frac{1-ct^{2}}{1-(c-\alpha)t}=\frac{(1-\sqrt{c}t)(1+\sqrt{c}t)}{1-(c-\alpha)t}\geq 1+\sqrt{c}t.

Denoting
h(u):=(1+\sqrt{c}u)e^{-\alpha u^{2}/2-u}
, it is enough to prove that
h(t)\geq 1=h(0)
, which will follow if we establish that
h^{\prime}(u)=\Big(\sqrt{c}(1-u-\alpha u^{2})-(\alpha u+1)\Big)e^{-\alpha u^{2}/2-u}\geq 0
on
(0,1/(c-\alpha))
, or equivalently that the quadratic function

\sqrt{c}(1-u-\alpha u^{2})-(\alpha u+1)
takes non-negative values at
u=0
and at
u=1/(c-\alpha)
. Indeed, its evaluation at
u=0
yields
\sqrt{c}-1=\sqrt{2(2+\alpha)}-1>0
, while its evaluation on
1/(c-\alpha)=1/(\alpha+4)
is lower bounded by
8/(\alpha+4)^{2}>0
.

Case of
t\in(\frac{1}{c-\alpha},+\infty)
.

Since
1-(c-\alpha)t<0
, we get that
\psi(t)\leq 0
as soon as

\frac{ct^{2}-1}{(c-\alpha)t-1}\leq e^{\alpha t^{2}/2+t}.

Since
t>1/(c-\alpha)
, we have
(c-\alpha)t>1
, and using (
136
) we get

\frac{\sqrt{c}t-1}{(c-\alpha)t-1}\leq\frac{\sqrt{c}t}{(c-\alpha)t}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Therefore

\frac{ct^{2}-1}{(c-\alpha)t-1}=\frac{(\sqrt{c}t-1)(\sqrt{c}t+1)}{(c-\alpha)t-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{c}t\leq\frac{e^{\alpha t^{2}/2+t}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{c}t}{2}.

Denoting
g(u):=ue^{-\alpha u^{2}/2-u}
, it is thus enough to show
that
\sqrt{c}g(t)\leq 1
to conclude. Since
g^{\prime}(u)=-(-1+u+\alpha u^{2})e^{-\alpha u^{2}/2-u}
,
the unique
u\geq 0
such that
g^{\prime}(u)=0
is
u_{\alpha}:=2/(\sqrt{4\alpha+1}+1)
, which satisfies
\alpha u_{\alpha}^{2}+u_{\alpha}-1=0
, and
the maximum of
g(u)
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
is at
u=u_{\alpha}
. As a result

\sqrt{c}g(t)\leq\sqrt{c}g(u_{\alpha})=2\frac{\sqrt{2(2+\alpha)}}{\sqrt{4\alpha+1}+1}e^{-1/2-u_{\alpha}/2}.

(137)

To conclude, we show that the r.h.s. is bounded by one, by distinguishing two cases.
On the one hand, if
\alpha\geq 2
, we have
2\sqrt{2(2+\alpha)}/(\sqrt{4\alpha+1}+1)\leq\sqrt{2}
, and since
u_{\alpha}\geq 0
the r.h.s. of (
137
) is upper bounded by
e^{-1/2}\sqrt{2}\leq 1
. On the other hand, if
\alpha\leq 2
, we
have
u_{\alpha}/2\geq 1/4
and
2\sqrt{2(2+\alpha)}/(\sqrt{4\alpha+1}+1)\leq 2
(the latter inequality holds for any

\alpha\geq 0
), so that the r.h.s. of (
137
) is upper bounded by
2e^{-3/4}\leq 1
. In both cases, we get as claimed that
\sqrt{c}g_{\alpha}(t)\leq 1
. ∎
